Implement the following plan:

# Rune v6.0 — Parallel Swarm Execution Implementation Plan

## Context

The swarm-execution-proposal-v2 collapses Rune's 5-service microservice architecture into a single-process "fat orchestrator" for local hardware. This plan implements the proposal across 5 phases (25–29), each on its own branch with granular commits, where every commit passes ruff, mypy, and pytest.

Key design decisions:
- **No new `libs/swarm` package** — swarm logic lives in `scripts/swarm.py` as described in the proposal. Supporting code (HardwareProbe, sandbox) goes into existing libs (`libs/shared`, `libs/model-training`).
- All GPU/system deps (psutil, pynvml) use INFRA-05 deferred-import pattern with `sys.modules` test injection.
- nsjail is Linux-only; macOS/CI falls back to subprocess (tested explicitly).
- AdapterRecord schema additions use `default=` values so `create_all()` works for new DBs.

---

After each phase branch, before merging:
```bash
uv run ruff check .
uv run ruff format --check .
uv run mypy services/*/src libs/*/src
uv run pytest --tb=short
uv run mkdocs build -f mkdocs.yml
```

Each commit within a phase must independently pass these checks.

## Comprehensive Rune Python Monorepo Exploration

I've thoroughly explored the Rune Python monorepo. Here's a complete analysis of its structure and architecture:

---

## **1. Directory Structure**

### Root Layout
- `/libs/` - 6 shared libraries using workspace
- `/services/` - 5 FastAPI microservices  
- `/scripts/` - Utility scripts (e2e_smoke.py, check-all.sh, etc.)
- `/tests/` - Integration tests
- `/docs/` - MkDocs documentation
- `/instructions/` - Planning and GSD context
- `.planning/` - Phase tracking (excluded from git)

---

## **2. Root pyproject.toml Analysis**

**File path:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/pyproject.toml`

**Key Features:**
- **uv workspace** with 8 members (6 libs + 2 services):
  - libs: shared, adapter-registry, evaluation, events-py, inference, model-training
  - services: api-service, evolution-svc, rune-agent, training-svc
  - Note: lora-server is Docker-only (not workspace member)

- **Root dependencies:** 31 packages including:
  - Core: aiohttp, httpx, fastapi, sqlalchemy, sqlmodel, langchain, langgraph
  - ML: openai, pytorch-related deps installed at root level for CI
  - Docs: mkdocs, mkdocs-monorepo-plugin, mkdocstrings

- **Dev dependencies:** mypy, pytest, ruff, pytest-cov, pytest-xdist (12 workers parallel)

- **Ruff config:** Python 3.12, line-length 88, strictness levels vary by file

- **mypy overrides:** Comprehensive list for GPU packages (torch, peft, safetensors, bitsandbytes, openai, trl, datasets, transformers) to avoid import failures in CI

- **pytest config:**
  - Paths: services/*/src, libs/*/src
  - addopts: `-n auto --import-mode=importlib` (12 parallel workers)
  - asyncio_mode: "auto"
  - pythonpath set for all library imports

---

## **3. Shared Library: libs/shared**

**Path:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/shared/src/shared/`

**__init__.py Exports:**
```python
from shared.rune_models import AdapterRef, CodingSession, EvolMetrics
def get_prompts_dir() -> Path  # Returns libs/shared/src/shared/templates/
```

**rune_models.py - 3 Pydantic Models:**

1. **AdapterRef** - Reference to a stored LoRA adapter
   - adapter_id: str (UUID)
   - task_type: str (e.g., "bug-fix")
   - fitness_score: Optional[float]

2. **CodingSession** - Complete agent coding session record
   - session_id: str
   - task_description: str
   - task_type: str
   - adapter_refs: list[AdapterRef]
   - attempt_count: int (0-indexed)
   - outcome: Optional[str] ("success", "exhausted", or None)

3. **EvolMetrics** - Evolution evaluation metrics
   - adapter_id: str
   - pass_rate: float (0.0-1.0)
   - fitness_score: float
   - generalization_delta: Optional[float]

---

## **4. Adapter Registry Library: libs/adapter-registry**

**Path:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/adapter-registry/src/adapter_registry/`

**models.py - AdapterRecord (SQLModel):**
```
Table: adapter_records
Primary key: id (str - UUID)

Tracked fields:
- version: int (lineage tracking)
- task_type: str (indexed)
- base_model_id: str (e.g., "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B")
- rank: int (LoRA rank)
- created_at: str (ISO 8601)
- file_path: str (adapter weights location)
- file_hash: str (SHA-256 for integrity)
- file_size_bytes: int
- pass_rate: Optional[float] (if evaluated)
- fitness_score: Optional[float] (if evaluated)
- source: str ("distillation", "evolution", "manual")
- session_id: str (producing session)
- is_archived: bool (soft delete flag)
```

**registry.py - AdapterRegistry Class:**

CRUD operations with SQLite via SQLModel:
- `__init__(engine)` - Activates WAL mode automatically on every connection
- `store(record)` - Persist new adapter, raises AdapterAlreadyExistsError on duplicate
- `retrieve_by_id(adapter_id)` - Get single adapter by ID
- `query_by_task_type(task_type)` - List all adapters by task type
- `list_all()` - List all non-archived adapters

Exception classes:
- AdapterAlreadyExistsError
- AdapterNotFoundError

---

## **5. Evaluation Library: libs/evaluation**

**Path:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/evaluation/src/evaluation/metrics.py`

**Implemented Functions:**

1. **calculate_pass_at_k** ✓ IMPLEMENTED
   - Unbiased Pass@k estimator from HumanEval paper
   - Formula: 1 - prod((n-c-i)/(n-i) for i in range(k))

2. **run_kill_switch_gate** ✓ IMPLEMENTED
   - Compares baseline vs adapter Pass@1
   - Returns: baseline_pass1, adapter_pass1, relative_delta, verdict ("PASS"/"FAIL")
   - threshold default: 0.05 (5% relative improvement)

3. **run_humaneval_subset** ✓ IMPLEMENTED
   - Bundles 20-task HumanEval subset
   - Takes completions dict {task_id -> completion_string}
   - Executes via subprocess, captures pass/fail
   - Returns: pass_count, fail_count, pass_rate, task_results, summary

4. **score_adapter_quality** - STUB (raises NotImplementedError)
   - Aggregates pass_rate + generalization_delta into single score

5. **compare_adapters** - STUB (raises NotImplementedError)
   - Head-to-head comparison of multiple adapters

6. **test_generalization** - STUB (raises NotImplementedError)
   - In-distribution vs OOD performance measurement

7. **evaluate_fitness** - STUB (raises NotImplementedError)
   - Evolutionary fitness composite score (pass_rate + diversity_score)

---

## **6. Model Training Library: libs/model-training**

**Path:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/`

**__init__.py Exports:**
```python
from model_training.trajectory import (
    format_for_sft, load_trajectory, record_trajectory
)
# GPU modules imported via submodule imports (deferred):
#   from model_training.peft_utils import build_qlora_config
#   from model_training.config import get_training_config
#   from model_training.trainer import train_qlora
#   from model_training.hypernetwork import DocToLoraHypernetwork
```

**trajectory.py - Trajectory Recording:**

1. **record_trajectory** ✓
   - Persists session data to `~/.rune/trajectories/{session_id}.json`
   - Respects RUNE_TRAJECTORY_DIR env var
   - Stores: steps list, task description, task type, adapter_ids, outcome, timestamp

2. **load_trajectory** ✓
   - Loads by session_id (filename without .json)
   - Raises FileNotFoundError if not found

3. **format_for_sft** ✓
   - Converts successful trajectories to SFT chat format
   - Only returns data if outcome == "success"
   - Extracts final successful step's generated_code
   - Returns: [{role: "system", ...}, {role: "user", ...}, {role: "assistant", ...}]

**hypernetwork.py - DocToLoraHypernetwork:**

Lazy-loaded Perceiver-based hypernetwork (deferred GPU import):

Architecture:
- Lightweight token embedding (not base model's)
- Learnable latents: nn.Parameter(num_latents=32, latent_dim=256)
- Cross-attention: latents attend over token embeddings
- Self-attention stack over latents (depth=4, heads=8)
- Linear projection head to all LoRA parameters

`save_hypernetwork_adapter(weights, output_dir, base_model_id)`:
- Writes adapter_model.safetensors
- Writes adapter_config.json (PEFT-compatible)
- target_modules: ["q_proj", "v_proj"] (no embed_tokens/lm_head per Phase 21-01)

---

## **7. Inference Library: libs/inference**

**Path:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/inference/src/inference/`

**__init__.py - Lazy Imports via __getattr__:**
```python
Exports:
- GenerationResult (dataclass)
- InferenceProvider (ABC)
- get_provider() function
- get_provider_for_step() function
- OllamaProvider (lazily loaded)
- VLLMProvider (lazily loaded)
```

**provider.py - Base Classes:**

`GenerationResult` (dataclass):
- text: str
- model: str
- adapter_id: str | None
- token_count: int
- finish_reason: str

`InferenceProvider` (ABC):
```python
async generate(prompt, model, adapter_id=None, max_tokens=1024) -> GenerationResult
async load_adapter(adapter_id, adapter_path) -> None
async unload_adapter(adapter_id) -> None
async list_adapters() -> list[str]
```

**factory.py - Provider Factory:**

Module-level cache: `_provider_cache: dict[(provider_type, base_url), InferenceProvider]`

`get_provider(provider_type=None, base_url=None)`:
- Defaults to "vllm" (from INFERENCE_PROVIDER env var)
- URLs: 
  - vLLM: http://localhost:8100/v1 (VLLM_BASE_URL env var)
  - Ollama: http://localhost:11434/v1 (OLLAMA_BASE_URL env var)
- Caches instances by (provider_type, base_url) tuple
- Lazy imports VLLMProvider or OllamaProvider on demand

`get_provider_for_step(step_config)`:
- Reads {"provider": "...", "base_url": "..."} from config
- Delegates to get_provider()

**conftest.py fixture:**
```python
@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def clear_provider_cache() -> None:
    from inference import factory
    factory._provider_cache.clear()
    yield
    factory._provider_cache.clear()
```

## **9. Services Overview**

### **9.1 Rune Agent Service: services/rune-agent**

**Path:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/services/rune-agent/src/rune_agent/`

**state.py - RuneState (TypedDict):**
```python
# Task intake
task_description: str
task_type: str
test_suite: str
adapter_ids: list[str]

# Loop tracking
session_id: str
attempt_count: int
max_attempts: int

# Per-attempt results
generated_code: str
stdout: str
stderr: str
exit_code: int
tests_passed: bool

# Accumulated trajectory
trajectory: list[dict[str, Any]]

# Terminal result
outcome: Optional[str]  # 'success' | 'exhausted' | None
```

**nodes.py - Async Node Functions:**

1. **generate_node(state) -> dict:**
   - Reads RUNE_MODEL env var (default: "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B")
   - Gets provider via `inference.get_provider()`
   - Builds prompt (first attempt vs retry with error context)
   - Returns: {generated_code: str}

2. **execute_node(state) -> dict:**
   - Reads RUNE_EXEC_TIMEOUT env var (default: 30s)
   - Runs code + test_suite in temp dir subprocess
   - Returns: {stdout, stderr, exit_code, tests_passed}

3. **reflect_node(state) -> dict:**
   - Records attempt in trajectory (immutable list concat, not append)
   - Increments attempt_count
   - Returns: {attempt_count, trajectory}

4. **save_trajectory_node(state) -> dict:**
   - Calls `record_trajectory()` from model_training
   - Sets outcome based on tests_passed
   - Returns: {outcome: "success" | "exhausted"}

**graph.py - LangGraph Workflow:**

`should_retry(state)` - Router logic:
- If tests_passed → "save_trajectory" (success)
- If attempt_count >= max_attempts → "save_trajectory" (exhausted)
- Else → "generate" (retry)

`create_graph()`:
```
START → generate → execute → reflect 
     ↓
     should_retry (conditional)
     ↓
     generate (retry loop) OR save_trajectory
     ↓
     END
```

`get_graph()` - Singleton pattern with global _graph variable

## **10. Test Patterns**

### **Root conftest.py: `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/conftest.py`**

Provides **factory fixtures** for all tests:

```python
@pytest.fixture
def make_adapter_record() -> Callable[..., AdapterRecord]
    # Defaults: id="test-adapter-001", version=1, task_type="bug-fix", etc.

@pytest.fixture
def make_adapter_ref() -> Callable[..., AdapterRef]
    # Defaults: adapter_id="test-adapter-001", task_type="bug-fix", fitness_score=None

@pytest.fixture
def make_coding_session() -> Callable[..., CodingSession]
    # Defaults: session_id="test-session-001", task_type="bug-fix", adapter_refs=[], etc.

@pytest.fixture
def make_training_job() -> Callable[..., TrainingJob]
    # Defaults: id="test-job-001", status="pending"

@pytest.fixture
def make_evolution_job() -> Callable[..., EvolutionJob]
    # Defaults: id="test-evol-job-001", status="pending"

@pytest.fixture
def make_evol_metrics() -> Callable[..., EvolMetrics]
    # Defaults: adapter_id="test-adapter-001", pass_rate=0.75, fitness_score=0.8
```

**Usage in tests:** No imports needed; pytest auto-discovers fixture.
```python
def test_something(make_adapter_record):
    record = make_adapter_record(task_type="code-gen")
    assert record.task_type == "code-gen"
```

### **sys.modules Injection Pattern**

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/tests/test_hypernetwork.py`

Pattern for testing GPU-dependent modules on CPU:

```python
def _inject_fake_hn_modules() -> None:
    """Create ModuleType fakes for torch, torch.nn, safetensors."""
    from types import ModuleType
    from unittest.mock import MagicMock
    
    fake_torch = ModuleType("torch")
    fake_torch.Tensor = MagicMock()
    fake_nn = ModuleType("torch.nn")
    fake_nn.Module = MagicMock()
    # ... etc
    
    sys.modules.setdefault("torch", fake_torch)
    sys.modules.setdefault("torch.nn", fake_nn)
    sys.modules.setdefault("safetensors", fake_safetensors)
    sys.modules.setdefault("safetensors.torch", fake_safetensors_torch)

requires_torch = pytest.mark.skipif(
    not _TORCH_AVAILABLE, reason="torch not installed"
)

@requires_torch
def test_with_real_torch():
    # Runs only if real torch available
```

## **12. Key Architectural Patterns**

### **Lazy Imports (INFRA-05)**
- GPU packages (torch, peft, safetensors) imported inside function bodies
- Enables CPU-only CI without installation failures
- Example: `from model_training.hypernetwork import DocToLoraHypernetwork` (proxy, not real class)

### **Provider Factory with Cache**
- Single source of truth for inference backends
- Cached by (provider_type, base_url) tuple
- Prevents config thrashing in tests

### **Immutable State in LangGraph**
- No in-place list modification (use `state["list"] + [item]`)
- Required by LangGraph's state management

### **Write-Ahead Logging (WAL) for SQLite**
- Activated automatically via SQLAlchemy event hook in AdapterRegistry.__init__
- Enables concurrent reads while writes in progress

### **Deferred GPU Module Loading**
- DocToLoraHypernetwork uses `_LazyHypernetworkProxy` pattern
- Actual nn.Module class only built on first instantiation
- Keeps module importable without torch

```
     1→"""Rune-specific Pydantic models for cross-service data contracts.
     2→
     3→Defines the shared data shapes for coding sessions, adapter references,
     4→and evolutionary fitness metrics used across all Rune services.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from typing import Optional
     8→
     9→from pydantic import BaseModel
    10→
    11→
    12→class AdapterRef(BaseModel):
    13→    """Reference to a stored LoRA adapter.
    14→
    15→    Used within CodingSession to track which adapters were loaded
    16→    during an agent coding session.
    17→
    18→    Attributes:
    19→        adapter_id: UUID of the adapter in the registry.
    20→        task_type: Task category this adapter was trained on (e.g. 'bug-fix').
    21→        fitness_score: Evolutionary fitness score, if evaluated.
    22→
    23→    Example:
    24→        >>> ref = AdapterRef(adapter_id="abc-123", task_type="bug-fix")
    25→        >>> ref.adapter_id
    26→        'abc-123'
    27→        >>> ref.fitness_score is None
    28→        True
    29→    """
    30→
    31→    adapter_id: str
    32→    task_type: str
    33→    fitness_score: Optional[float] = None
    34→
    35→
    36→class CodingSession(BaseModel):
    37→    """A complete agent coding session record.
    38→
    39→    Tracks the full lifecycle of a Rune agent session including which
    40→    adapters were used, how many generate-execute-reflect cycles occurred,
    41→    and the final outcome.
    42→
    43→    Attributes:
    44→        session_id: Unique session identifier.
    45→        task_description: Human-readable description of the coding task.
    46→        task_type: Task category (e.g. 'bug-fix', 'feature-impl').
    47→        adapter_refs: List of adapters loaded during this session.
    48→        attempt_count: Number of generate-execute-reflect cycles completed.
    49→        outcome: Final session result ('success', 'exhausted', or None if in progress).
    50→
    51→    Example:
    52→        >>> session = CodingSession(
    53→        ...     session_id="sess-001",
    54→        ...     task_description="Fix import error",
    55→        ...     task_type="bug-fix",
    56→        ... )
    57→        >>> session.adapter_refs
    58→        []
    59→        >>> session.attempt_count
    60→        0
    61→        >>> session.outcome is None
    62→        True
    63→    """
    64→
    65→    session_id: str
    66→    task_description: str
    67→    task_type: str
    68→    adapter_refs: list[AdapterRef] = []
    69→    attempt_count: int = 0
    70→    outcome: Optional[str] = None
    71→
    72→
    73→class EvolMetrics(BaseModel):
    74→    """Evolution evaluation metrics for an adapter.
    75→
    76→    Captures the performance and fitness measurements used by the
    77→    evolution service to decide which adapters to keep, mutate, or retire.
    78→
    79→    Attributes:
    80→        adapter_id: UUID of the adapter being evaluated.
    81→        pass_rate: Pass rate on benchmark tasks (0.0 to 1.0).
    82→        fitness_score: Overall evolutionary fitness score.
    83→        generalization_delta: Difference between in-distribution and OOD performance.
    84→
    85→    Example:
    86→        >>> metrics = EvolMetrics(
    87→        ...     adapter_id="adapter-001",
    88→        ...     pass_rate=0.85,
    89→        ...     fitness_score=0.9,
    90→        ... )
    91→        >>> metrics.generalization_delta is None
    92→        True
    93→    """
    94→
    95→    adapter_id: str
    96→    pass_rate: float
    97→    fitness_score: float
    98→    generalization_delta: Optional[float] = None
    99→
```

```
     1→"""Node functions for the Rune coding agent recursive loop."""
     2→
     3→import logging
     4→import os
     5→import re
     6→import subprocess
     7→import tempfile
     8→from typing import Any
     9→
    10→from inference import GenerationResult, get_provider
    11→from model_training.trajectory import record_trajectory
    12→
    13→from .state import RuneState
    14→
    15→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    16→
    17→SYSTEM_PROMPT = "You are a Python code generator. Output only code, no explanation."
    18→DEFAULT_TIMEOUT = 30
    19→DEFAULT_MODEL = "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B"
    20→
    21→
    22→def _build_prompt(state: RuneState) -> str:
    23→    """Build the user prompt for the LLM based on current attempt.
    24→
    25→    First attempt (attempt_count == 0): includes task description and test suite.
    26→    Retry (attempt_count > 0): also includes prior code, stdout, stderr, exit code.
    27→
    28→    Args:
    29→        state: Current agent state.
    30→
    31→    Returns:
    32→        Formatted prompt string.
    33→    """
    34→    task = state["task_description"]
    35→    test_suite = state["test_suite"]
    36→
    37→    base = (
    38→        f"Task: {task}\n\n"
    39→        f"Test suite (your code must pass these):\n{test_suite}\n\n"
    40→        "Write a Python solution:"
    41→    )
    42→
    43→    if state["attempt_count"] == 0:
    44→        return base
    45→
    46→    prior_code = state["generated_code"]
    47→    stdout = state["stdout"]
    48→    stderr = state["stderr"]
    49→    exit_code = state["exit_code"]
    50→
    51→    return (
    52→        f"{base}\n\n"
    53→        "Your previous attempt produced the following errors:\n"
    54→        f"Code:\n{prior_code}\n\n"
    55→        f"stdout:\n{stdout}\n"
    56→        f"stderr:\n{stderr}\n"
    57→        f"exit_code: {exit_code}\n\n"
    58→        "Please fix the issues and write a corrected solution:"
    59→    )
    60→
    61→
    62→def _extract_code(text: str) -> str:
    63→    """Extract code from a markdown python block, or return stripped text.
    64→
    65→    Args:
    66→        text: Raw LLM response text.
    67→
    68→    Returns:
    69→        Extracted code string.
    70→    """
    71→    match = re.search(r"```python\s*(.*?)```", text, re.DOTALL)
    72→    if match:
    73→        return match.group(1).strip()
    74→    return text.strip()
    75→
    76→
    77→async def generate_node(state: RuneState) -> dict[str, Any]:
    78→    """Generate code for the given task using the inference layer.
    79→
    80→    Calls the LLM (with optional LoRA adapters) to produce a code solution
    81→    for the task description.
    82→
    83→    Args:
    84→        state: Current agent state with task description and context.
    85→
    86→    Returns:
    87→        State update dict with generated_code key.
    88→
    89→    Example:
    90→        >>> state = {"task_description": "Write fibonacci", "task_type": "function",
    91→        ...          "test_suite": "assert fib(5) == 5", "adapter_ids": []}
    92→        >>> result = await generate_node(state)
    93→        >>> 'generated_code' in result
    94→        True
    95→    """
    96→    # Read env vars inside function body so monkeypatch.setenv() works in tests
    97→    model: str = os.environ.get("RUNE_MODEL", DEFAULT_MODEL)
    98→    adapter_id: str | None = state["adapter_ids"][0] if state["adapter_ids"] else None
    99→
   100→    provider = get_provider()
   101→    user_prompt = _build_prompt(state)
   102→    full_prompt = f"{SYSTEM_PROMPT}\n\n{user_prompt}"
   103→
   104→    result: GenerationResult = await provider.generate(
   105→        prompt=full_prompt,
   106→        model=model,
   107→        adapter_id=adapter_id,
   108→    )
   109→
   110→    extracted = _extract_code(result.text)
   111→    logger.info(
   112→        "generate_node: attempt=%d, model=%s, adapter_id=%s, tokens=%d",
   113→        state["attempt_count"],
   114→        result.model,
   115→        result.adapter_id,
   116→        result.token_count,
   117→    )
   118→
   119→    return {"generated_code": extracted}
   120→
   121→
   122→async def execute_node(state: RuneState) -> dict[str, Any]:
   123→    """Execute the generated code in a sandboxed environment.
   124→
   125→    Runs the generated code against the test suite in an isolated subprocess
   126→    and captures stdout, stderr, and exit code.
   127→
   128→    Args:
   129→        state: Current agent state with generated code and test suite.
   130→
   131→    Returns:
   132→        State update dict with stdout, stderr, exit_code, and tests_passed keys.
   133→
   134→    Example:
   135→        >>> state = {"generated_code": "def fib(n): return n", "test_suite": ""}
   136→        >>> result = await execute_node(state)
   137→        >>> 'tests_passed' in result
   138→        True
   139→    """
   140→    # Read env var inside function body so monkeypatch.setenv() works in tests
   141→    timeout: int = int(os.environ.get("RUNE_EXEC_TIMEOUT", DEFAULT_TIMEOUT))
   142→
   143→    script = state["generated_code"] + "\n\n" + state["test_suite"]
   144→
   145→    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
   146→        script_path = tmpdir + "/solution.py"
   147→        with open(script_path, "w") as f:
   148→            f.write(script)
   149→
   150→        try:
   151→            proc = subprocess.run(
   152→                ["python", script_path],
   153→                capture_output=True,
   154→                text=True,
   155→                timeout=timeout,
   156→                cwd=tmpdir,
   157→            )
   158→            stdout = proc.stdout
   159→            stderr = proc.stderr
   160→            exit_code = proc.returncode
   161→            tests_passed = proc.returncode == 0
   162→        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
   163→            stdout = ""
   164→            stderr = f"Execution timed out after {timeout}s"
   165→            exit_code = 1
   166→            tests_passed = False
   167→
   168→    logger.info(
   169→        "execute_node: exit_code=%d, tests_passed=%s",
   170→        exit_code,
   171→        tests_passed,
   172→    )
   173→
   174→    return {
   175→        "stdout": stdout,
   176→        "stderr": stderr,
   177→        "exit_code": exit_code,
   178→        "tests_passed": tests_passed,
   179→    }
   180→
   181→
   182→async def reflect_node(state: RuneState) -> dict[str, Any]:
   183→    """Reflect on execution results and record the attempt in trajectory.
   184→
   185→    Increments the attempt counter and appends the current attempt's data
   186→    to the trajectory list. Does not make any LLM calls.
   187→
   188→    Args:
   189→        state: Current agent state with execution results.
   190→
   191→    Returns:
   192→        State update dict with incremented attempt_count and extended trajectory.
   193→
   194→    Example:
   195→        >>> state = {"attempt_count": 0, "generated_code": "def fib(n): pass",
   196→        ...          "exit_code": 1, "tests_passed": False, "trajectory": [],
   197→        ...          "stdout": "", "stderr": ""}
   198→        >>> result = await reflect_node(state)
   199→        >>> result['attempt_count']
   200→        1
   201→    """
   202→    step: dict[str, Any] = {
   203→        "generated_code": state["generated_code"],
   204→        "stdout": state["stdout"],
   205→        "stderr": state["stderr"],
   206→        "exit_code": state["exit_code"],
   207→        "tests_passed": state["tests_passed"],
   208→    }
   209→
   210→    # Use list concatenation (not .append()) — LangGraph requires immutable state
   211→    new_trajectory: list[dict[str, Any]] = state["trajectory"] + [step]
   212→    new_attempt_count = state["attempt_count"] + 1
   213→
   214→    logger.info(
   215→        "reflect_node: attempt=%d -> %d, trajectory_length=%d",
   216→        state["attempt_count"],
   217→        new_attempt_count,
   218→        len(new_trajectory),
   219→    )
   220→
   221→    return {
   222→        "attempt_count": new_attempt_count,
   223→        "trajectory": new_trajectory,
   224→    }
   225→
   226→
   227→async def save_trajectory_node(state: RuneState) -> dict[str, Any]:
   228→    """Save the completed trajectory for parametric memory training.
   229→
   230→    Persists the trajectory to disk via record_trajectory() and determines
   231→    the final outcome based on whether tests passed.
   232→
   233→    Args:
   234→        state: Current agent state with complete trajectory and outcome.
   235→
   236→    Returns:
   237→        State update dict with outcome key ('success' or 'exhausted').
   238→
   239→    Example:
   240→        >>> state = {"tests_passed": True, "session_id": "abc", "trajectory": [],
   241→        ...          "task_description": "", "task_type": "", "adapter_ids": []}
   242→        >>> result = await save_trajectory_node(state)
   243→        >>> result['outcome']
   244→        'success'
   245→    """
   246→    outcome = "success" if state["tests_passed"] else "exhausted"
   247→
   248→    record_trajectory(
   249→        session_id=state["session_id"],
   250→        steps=state["trajectory"],
   251→        outcome=outcome,
   252→        task_description=state["task_description"],
   253→        task_type=state["task_type"],
   254→        adapter_ids=state["adapter_ids"],
   255→    )
   256→
   257→    logger.info(
   258→        "save_trajectory_node: session_id=%s, outcome=%s",
   259→        state["session_id"],
   260→        outcome,
   261→    )
   262→
   263→    return {"outcome": outcome}
   264→
```

```
     1→"""SQLModel table model for LoRA adapter metadata records."""
     2→
     3→from typing import Optional
     4→
     5→from sqlmodel import Field, SQLModel
     6→
     7→
     8→class AdapterRecord(SQLModel, table=True):
     9→    """A stored LoRA adapter metadata record.
    10→
    11→    Tracks metadata for a single LoRA adapter including its lineage,
    12→    storage location, and evaluation metrics. Backed by SQLite via SQLModel.
    13→
    14→    Attributes:
    15→        id: Unique adapter identifier (UUID string).
    16→        version: Adapter version number for lineage tracking.
    17→        task_type: Task category this adapter was trained on (e.g. 'bug-fix').
    18→        base_model_id: Identifier of the base model this adapter was trained from.
    19→        rank: LoRA rank used during training.
    20→        created_at: ISO 8601 timestamp of adapter creation.
    21→        file_path: Filesystem path to the adapter weights file.
    22→        file_hash: SHA-256 hash of the adapter weights file for integrity checks.
    23→        file_size_bytes: Size of the adapter weights file in bytes.
    24→        pass_rate: Pass rate on benchmark tasks (0.0 to 1.0), if evaluated.
    25→        fitness_score: Overall evolutionary fitness score, if evaluated.
    26→        source: How the adapter was created ('distillation', 'evolution', 'manual').
    27→        session_id: ID of the coding session that produced this adapter.
    28→        is_archived: Whether this adapter has been archived (soft delete).
    29→
    30→    Example:
    31→        >>> record = AdapterRecord(
    32→        ...     id="adapter-001", version=1, task_type="bug-fix",
    33→        ...     base_model_id="Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B", rank=16,
    34→        ...     created_at="2026-01-01T00:00:00Z",
    35→        ...     file_path="/adapters/adapter-001.safetensors",
    36→        ...     file_hash="abc123", file_size_bytes=1024,
    37→        ...     source="distillation", session_id="sess-001",
    38→        ... )
    39→        >>> record.task_type
    40→        'bug-fix'
    41→        >>> record.is_archived
    42→        False
    43→    """
    44→
    45→    __tablename__ = "adapter_records"
    46→
    47→    id: str = Field(primary_key=True)
    48→    version: int
    49→    task_type: str = Field(index=True)
    50→    base_model_id: str
    51→    rank: int
    52→    created_at: str
    53→    file_path: str
    54→    file_hash: str
    55→    file_size_bytes: int
    56→    pass_rate: Optional[float] = Field(default=None)
    57→    fitness_score: Optional[float] = Field(default=None)
    58→    source: str
    59→    session_id: str
    60→    is_archived: bool = Field(default=False)
    61→
```

```
     1→"""AdapterRegistry class providing CRUD operations for adapter metadata."""
     2→
     3→from sqlalchemy import event
     4→from sqlalchemy.engine import Engine
     5→from sqlmodel import Session, SQLModel, select
     6→
     7→from adapter_registry.exceptions import AdapterAlreadyExistsError, AdapterNotFoundError
     8→from adapter_registry.models import AdapterRecord
     9→
    10→
    11→class AdapterRegistry:
    12→    """Registry for storing and querying LoRA adapter metadata.
    13→
    14→    Provides CRUD operations backed by SQLite via SQLModel. The registry
    15→    is initialized with a SQLAlchemy Engine and creates tables idempotently
    16→    on construction. WAL mode is activated automatically on every new
    17→    SQLite connection via an event hook registered before table creation.
    18→
    19→    Raises:
    20→        AdapterAlreadyExistsError: When storing a duplicate adapter ID.
    21→        AdapterNotFoundError: When querying for a non-existent adapter.
    22→
    23→    Example:
    24→        >>> from sqlalchemy import create_engine
    25→        >>> engine = create_engine("sqlite:///adapters.db")
    26→        >>> registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=engine)
    27→        >>> registry.store(record)
    28→    """
    29→
    30→    def __init__(self, engine: Engine) -> None:
    31→        """Initialize the registry with a SQLAlchemy Engine.
    32→
    33→        Registers a WAL-mode hook on the engine before creating tables to
    34→        ensure every connection (including those opened by create_all) uses
    35→        Write-Ahead Logging. Table creation is idempotent — safe to call
    36→        against an existing database.
    37→
    38→        Args:
    39→            engine: A SQLAlchemy Engine connected to the target SQLite database.
    40→        """
    41→        self._engine = engine
    42→
    43→        @event.listens_for(engine, "connect")
    44→        def _set_wal(dbapi_conn, _record):  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    45→            dbapi_conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL")
    46→
    47→        SQLModel.metadata.create_all(engine)
    48→
    49→    def store(self, record: AdapterRecord) -> None:
    50→        """Store a new adapter record in the registry.
    51→
    52→        Args:
    53→            record: The AdapterRecord to persist.
    54→
    55→        Raises:
    56→            AdapterAlreadyExistsError: If an adapter with the same ID exists.
    57→
    58→        Example:
    59→            >>> engine = create_engine("sqlite:///adapters.db")
    60→            >>> registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=engine)
    61→            >>> record = AdapterRecord(id="abc-1", version=1, task_type="bug-fix",
    62→            ...     base_model_id="qwen2.5-coder-7b", rank=16,
    63→            ...     created_at="2026-01-01T00:00:00Z",
    64→            ...     file_path="/adapters/abc-1.safetensors",
    65→            ...     file_hash="sha256hash", file_size_bytes=4096,
    66→            ...     source="distillation", session_id="sess-001")
    67→            >>> registry.store(record)
    68→        """
    69→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
    70→            if session.get(AdapterRecord, record.id) is not None:
    71→                raise AdapterAlreadyExistsError(
    72→                    f"Adapter '{record.id}' already exists."
    73→                )
    74→            session.add(record)
    75→            session.commit()
    76→            session.expunge(record)
    77→
    78→    def retrieve_by_id(self, adapter_id: str) -> AdapterRecord:
    79→        """Retrieve a single adapter record by its unique ID.
    80→
    81→        Args:
    82→            adapter_id: The unique identifier of the adapter to retrieve.
    83→
    84→        Returns:
    85→            The matching AdapterRecord.
    86→
    87→        Raises:
    88→            AdapterNotFoundError: If no adapter with the given ID exists.
    89→
    90→        Example:
    91→            >>> engine = create_engine("sqlite:///adapters.db")
    92→            >>> registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=engine)
    93→            >>> record = registry.retrieve_by_id("abc-1")
    94→        """
    95→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
    96→            record = session.get(AdapterRecord, adapter_id)
    97→            if record is None:
    98→                raise AdapterNotFoundError(f"No adapter with id '{adapter_id}'.")
    99→            session.expunge(record)
   100→            return record
   101→
   102→    def query_by_task_type(self, task_type: str) -> list[AdapterRecord]:
   103→        """Query all adapters matching a given task type.
   104→
   105→        Args:
   106→            task_type: The task category to filter by (e.g. 'bug-fix').
   107→
   108→        Returns:
   109→            List of matching AdapterRecord instances. Empty list if none match.
   110→
   111→        Example:
   112→            >>> engine = create_engine("sqlite:///adapters.db")
   113→            >>> registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=engine)
   114→            >>> results = registry.query_by_task_type("bug-fix")
   115→        """
   116→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
   117→            stmt = select(AdapterRecord).where(AdapterRecord.task_type == task_type)
   118→            results = list(session.exec(stmt).all())
   119→            for r in results:
   120→                session.expunge(r)
   121→            return results
   122→
   123→    def list_all(self) -> list[AdapterRecord]:
   124→        """List all non-archived adapter records.
   125→
   126→        Returns:
   127→            List of all AdapterRecord instances where is_archived=False.
   128→
   129→        Example:
   130→            >>> engine = create_engine("sqlite:///adapters.db")
   131→            >>> registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=engine)
   132→            >>> all_adapters = registry.list_all()
   133→        """
   134→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
   135→            stmt = select(AdapterRecord).where(
   136→                AdapterRecord.is_archived == False  # noqa: E712
   137→            )
   138→            results = list(session.exec(stmt).all())
   139→            for r in results:
   140→                session.expunge(r)
   141→            return results
   142→
```

```
     1→"""Evaluation metrics for adapter benchmarking and fitness scoring.
     2→
     3→Provides functions for running benchmarks (HumanEval), calculating metrics
     4→(Pass@k, quality scores), comparing adapters, testing generalization,
     5→and computing evolutionary fitness for the evolution operator.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→import json
    11→import math
    12→import subprocess
    13→import sys
    14→import tempfile
    15→from pathlib import Path
    16→from typing import Any, Optional
    17→
    18→_DATA_DIR = Path(__file__).parent / "data"
    19→
    20→
    21→def calculate_pass_at_k(n_samples: int, n_correct: int, k: int = 1) -> float:
    22→    """Calculate the Pass@k metric for code generation evaluation.
    23→
    24→    Computes the probability that at least one of k sampled solutions is
    25→    correct, using the unbiased estimator from the HumanEval paper
    26→    (Chen et al., 2021). This avoids the high-variance naive estimator.
    27→
    28→    Formula: 1 - prod((n-c-i)/(n-i) for i in range(k))
    29→
    30→    Args:
    31→        n_samples: Total number of samples generated per problem.
    32→        n_correct: Number of correct samples out of n_samples.
    33→        k: Number of attempts allowed for the Pass@k metric. Defaults to 1.
    34→
    35→    Returns:
    36→        Pass@k probability as a float between 0.0 and 1.0.
    37→
    38→    Raises:
    39→        ValueError: If n_correct > n_samples.
    40→
    41→    Example:
    42→        >>> score = calculate_pass_at_k(n_samples=100, n_correct=85, k=1)
    43→        >>> score
    44→        0.85
    45→    """
    46→    if n_correct > n_samples:
    47→        raise ValueError(
    48→            f"n_correct ({n_correct}) cannot be greater than n_samples ({n_samples})"
    49→        )
    50→    if n_correct == n_samples:
    51→        return 1.0
    52→    if n_samples - n_correct < k:
    53→        return 1.0
    54→    # Unbiased estimator: 1 - prod((n-c-i)/(n-i) for i in range(k))
    55→    product = math.prod((n_samples - n_correct - i) / (n_samples - i) for i in range(k))
    56→    return 1.0 - product
    57→
    58→
    59→def run_kill_switch_gate(
    60→    baseline_pass1: float,
    61→    adapter_pass1: float,
    62→    threshold: float = 0.05,
    63→) -> dict[str, object]:
    64→    """Compare baseline vs adapter Pass@1 scores and return a PASS/FAIL verdict.
    65→
    66→    Computes the relative improvement of the adapter over the baseline and
    67→    returns PASS if the improvement meets the threshold, FAIL otherwise.
    68→
    69→    Args:
    70→        baseline_pass1: Pass@1 score for the baseline model (no adapter).
    71→        adapter_pass1: Pass@1 score for the adapter model.
    72→        threshold: Minimum required relative improvement (default 0.05 = 5%).
    73→
    74→    Returns:
    75→        Dictionary with:
    76→            - "baseline_pass1": float
    77→            - "adapter_pass1": float
    78→            - "relative_delta": float, relative improvement over baseline
    79→            - "verdict": str, "PASS" or "FAIL"
    80→
    81→    Example:
    82→        >>> result = run_kill_switch_gate(0.50, 0.55)
    83→        >>> result["verdict"]
    84→        'PASS'
    85→    """
    86→    # 1e-9 prevents division-by-zero when baseline is 0.0
    87→    relative_delta = (adapter_pass1 - baseline_pass1) / max(baseline_pass1, 1e-9)
    88→    verdict = "PASS" if adapter_pass1 >= baseline_pass1 * (1 + threshold) else "FAIL"
    89→
    90→    print(
    91→        f"Kill-switch gate result: {verdict}\n"
    92→        f"  Baseline  Pass@1: {baseline_pass1:.4f}\n"
    93→        f"  Adapter   Pass@1: {adapter_pass1:.4f}\n"
    94→        f"  Relative delta:   {relative_delta:+.2%} (threshold: {threshold:+.0%})"
    95→    )
    96→
    97→    return {
    98→        "baseline_pass1": baseline_pass1,
    99→        "adapter_pass1": adapter_pass1,
   100→        "relative_delta": relative_delta,
   101→        "verdict": verdict,
   102→    }
   103→
   104→
   105→def run_humaneval_subset(
   106→    adapter_id: Optional[str],
   107→    subset_size: int = 20,
   108→    model: str = "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B",
   109→    completions: Optional[dict[str, str]] = None,
   110→) -> dict[str, Any]:
   111→    """Run a HumanEval benchmark subset to evaluate an adapter.
   112→
   113→    Loads tasks from the bundled 20-task HumanEval subset JSON and evaluates
   114→    them using the provided completions. For each task, concatenates
   115→    prompt + completion + test + check(entry_point), writes to a temp script,
   116→    and executes via subprocess. Exit code 0 = passed.
   117→
   118→    Args:
   119→        adapter_id: UUID of the adapter to test (None = baseline, no adapter).
   120→            Currently informational; inference wiring happens at a higher level.
   121→        subset_size: Ignored — always uses the fixed 20-task bundled subset.
   122→        model: Base model name (informational only, not used for inference here).
   123→        completions: Dict mapping task_id -> completion string. Only tasks with
   124→            an entry in this dict are evaluated. If None or empty, returns empty
   125→            results.
   126→
   127→    Returns:
   128→        Dictionary with benchmark results including:
   129→            - "pass_count": int, number of tasks passed
   130→            - "fail_count": int, number of tasks failed
   131→            - "pass_rate": float, fraction of tasks passed (0.0 to 1.0)
   132→            - "task_results": list of per-task result dicts with task_id, passed
   133→            - "summary": str, human-readable summary
   134→
   135→    Raises:
   136→        NotImplementedError: If completions are not provided (inference not wired).
   137→
   138→    Example:
   139→        >>> completions = {"HumanEval/0": "    return []"}
   140→        >>> results = run_humaneval_subset(adapter_id=None, completions=completions)
   141→        >>> results["pass_count"]
   142→        0
   143→    """
   144→    if completions is None:
   145→        raise NotImplementedError("run_humaneval_subset is not yet implemented.")
   146→
   147→    # Load bundled task data
   148→    subset_path = _DATA_DIR / "humaneval_subset.json"
   149→    with subset_path.open() as f:
   150→        all_tasks: list[dict[str, str]] = json.load(f)
   151→
   152→    # Build lookup by task_id
   153→    task_map = {t["task_id"]: t for t in all_tasks}
   154→
   155→    task_results: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
   156→
   157→    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
   158→        for task_id, completion in completions.items():
   159→            task = task_map.get(task_id)
   160→            if task is None:
   161→                continue
   162→
   163→            # Build executable script: prompt + completion + test + check(entry_point)
   164→            script = (
   165→                task["prompt"]
   166→                + completion
   167→                + "\n"
   168→                + task["test"]
   169→                + f"\ncheck({task['entry_point']})\n"
   170→            )
   171→
   172→            script_path = Path(tmpdir) / f"{task_id.replace('/', '_')}.py"
   173→            script_path.write_text(script)
   174→
   175→            proc = subprocess.run(
   176→                [sys.executable, str(script_path)],
   177→                capture_output=True,
   178→                text=True,
   179→                timeout=30,
   180→                cwd=tmpdir,
   181→            )
   182→            passed = proc.returncode == 0
   183→            task_results.append({"task_id": task_id, "passed": passed})
   184→
   185→    pass_count = sum(1 for r in task_results if r["passed"])
   186→    fail_count = len(task_results) - pass_count
   187→    total = len(task_results)
   188→    pass_rate = pass_count / total if total > 0 else 0.0
   189→
   190→    summary = (
   191→        f"HumanEval subset: {pass_count}/{total} passed "
   192→        f"(pass_rate={pass_rate:.2%}, adapter_id={adapter_id})"
   193→    )
   194→    print(summary)
   195→
   196→    return {
   197→        "pass_count": pass_count,
   198→        "fail_count": fail_count,
   199→        "pass_rate": pass_rate,
   200→        "task_results": task_results,
   201→        "summary": summary,
   202→    }
   203→
   204→
   205→def score_adapter_quality(
   206→    adapter_id: str,
   207→    pass_rate: float,
   208→    generalization_delta: float | None = None,
   209→) -> float:
   210→    """Compute an overall quality score for an adapter.
   211→
   212→    Aggregates the adapter's benchmark pass rate with its generalization
   213→    performance (if available) into a single scalar quality score. When
   214→    generalization data is absent, quality is derived from pass rate alone.
   215→
   216→    Args:
   217→        adapter_id: UUID of the adapter to score.
   218→        pass_rate: Fraction of benchmark tasks passed, in range 0.0 to 1.0.
   219→        generalization_delta: Optional difference between in-distribution and
   220→            out-of-distribution (OOD) performance. Positive values indicate
   221→            the adapter generalizes well; negative values indicate overfitting.
   222→            Defaults to None (not measured).
   223→
   224→    Returns:
   225→        Quality score as a float between 0.0 and 1.0. Higher values indicate
   226→        a better overall adapter quality.
   227→
   228→    Raises:
   229→        NotImplementedError: score_adapter_quality is not yet implemented.
   230→
   231→    Example:
   232→        >>> score = score_adapter_quality("adapter-001", pass_rate=0.85)
   233→        >>> score
   234→        0.85
   235→        >>> score_with_gen = score_adapter_quality(
   236→        ...     "adapter-001", 0.85, generalization_delta=0.1,
   237→        ... )
   238→        >>> score_with_gen > score
   239→        True

   278→

   280→def test_generalization(

   282→    in_distribution_tasks: list[str] | None = None,

   284→) -> dict[str, Any]:
   285→    """Test whether an adapter generalizes beyond its training distribution.
   286→
   287→    Evaluates the adapter on both in-distribution tasks (matching the training

   291→    Args:

   298→    Returns:

   301→            - "ood_score": float, performance on out-of-distribution tasks
```

```
     1→"""LoRA server configuration for vLLM-based inference."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass, fields
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→from typing import Any
     8→
     9→import yaml
    10→
    11→
    12→@dataclass
    13→class LoraServerConfig:
    14→    """Configuration for the vLLM-based LoRA inference server.
    15→
    16→    Default settings target single-GPU operation; adjust
    17→    ``pipeline_parallel_size`` and ``tensor_parallel_size`` for multi-GPU
    18→    setups.
    19→
    20→    Warning:
    21→        Tensor parallelism (``tensor_parallel_size > 1``) combined with LoRA
    22→        adapter serving may produce corrupted outputs on consumer GPUs without
    23→        NVLink (vLLM issue #21471). If you have NVLink-equipped GPUs, TP is
    24→        viable — verify output correctness with a known-good adapter before
    25→        using in production. For PCIe-connected GPUs, pipeline parallelism
    26→        is the recommended multi-GPU strategy.
    27→
    28→    Attributes:
    29→        model: HuggingFace model identifier.
    30→        pipeline_parallel_size: Number of pipeline parallel stages. Set to 1
    31→            for single-GPU operation (default). Set to N for N-GPU pipeline
    32→            parallelism.
    33→        tensor_parallel_size: Number of tensor parallel shards. Defaults to 1.
    34→            See warning above before enabling TP with LoRA on consumer GPUs.
    35→        enable_lora: Whether to enable LoRA adapter support.
    36→        quantization: Quantization method (e.g. 'awq').
    37→        port: Port for the vLLM inference server.
    38→        health_port: Port for the health sidecar.
    39→        max_loras: Maximum number of concurrent LoRA adapters.
    40→        max_lora_rank: Maximum rank for LoRA adapters (capped at 64 to avoid OOM).
    41→        gpu_memory_utilization: Fraction of GPU VRAM for the model (0.80 leaves
    42→            headroom for LoRA).
    43→
    44→    Example:
    45→        >>> config = LoraServerConfig()
    46→        >>> config.pipeline_parallel_size
    47→        1
    48→        >>> config.tensor_parallel_size
    49→        1
    50→    """
    51→
    52→    model: str = "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct"
    53→    pipeline_parallel_size: int = 1
    54→    tensor_parallel_size: int = 1
    55→    enable_lora: bool = True
    56→    quantization: str = "awq"
    57→    port: int = 8000
    58→    health_port: int = 8001
    59→    max_loras: int = 8
    60→    max_lora_rank: int = 64
    61→    gpu_memory_utilization: float = 0.80
    62→
    63→    @classmethod
    64→    def from_yaml(cls, path: str) -> LoraServerConfig:
    65→        """Load configuration from a YAML file.
    66→
    67→        Only keys matching dataclass fields are passed through;
    68→        unknown keys in the YAML are silently ignored.
    69→
    70→        Args:
    71→            path: Filesystem path to the YAML config file.
    72→
    73→        Returns:
    74→            A LoraServerConfig populated from the YAML file.
    75→
    76→        Raises:
    77→            FileNotFoundError: If the YAML file does not exist.
    78→
    79→        Example:
    80→            >>> config = LoraServerConfig.from_yaml("config.yml")
    81→            >>> config.model
    82→            'Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct'
    83→        """
    84→        raw: dict[str, Any] = yaml.safe_load(Path(path).read_text())
    85→        valid_fields = {f.name for f in fields(cls)}
    86→        filtered = {k: v for k, v in raw.items() if k in valid_fields}
    87→        return cls(**filtered)
    88→
```

```
     1→"""Tests for AdapterRegistry CRUD methods.
     2→
     3→These tests verify the full implementation: constructor, WAL mode, and all
     4→four CRUD methods with their expected behaviors including error cases.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→import pytest
     8→from adapter_registry import AdapterRegistry
     9→from adapter_registry.exceptions import (
    10→    AdapterAlreadyExistsError,
    11→    AdapterNotFoundError,
    12→)
    13→from sqlalchemy import create_engine
    14→from sqlalchemy.engine import Engine
    15→
    16→
    17→@pytest.fixture
    18→def engine(tmp_path) -> Engine:
    19→    """Create an in-memory SQLite engine for test isolation."""
    20→    db_path = tmp_path / "test_registry.db"
    21→    return create_engine(f"sqlite:///{db_path}")
    22→
    23→
    24→@pytest.fixture
    25→def registry(engine) -> AdapterRegistry:
    26→    """Create an AdapterRegistry instance with the test engine."""
    27→    return AdapterRegistry(engine=engine)
    28→
    29→
    30→# --- Constructor ---
    31→
    32→
    33→def test_constructor_requires_engine(engine) -> None:
    34→    """AdapterRegistry accepts an Engine parameter and creates tables."""
    35→    registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=engine)
    36→    assert registry is not None
    37→
    38→
    39→# --- store() ---
    40→
    41→
    42→def test_store_persists_record(registry, make_adapter_record) -> None:
    43→    """store() persists a record that can be retrieved by id."""
    44→    record = make_adapter_record()
    45→    registry.store(record)
    46→    retrieved = registry.retrieve_by_id(record.id)
    47→    assert retrieved.id == record.id
    48→
    49→
    50→def test_store_raises_on_duplicate_id(registry, make_adapter_record) -> None:
    51→    """store() raises AdapterAlreadyExistsError on duplicate ID."""
    52→    record = make_adapter_record()
    53→    registry.store(record)
    54→    with pytest.raises(AdapterAlreadyExistsError):
    55→        registry.store(make_adapter_record())  # same id="test-adapter-001"
    56→
    57→
    58→def test_store_returns_none(registry, make_adapter_record) -> None:
    59→    """store() returns None on success."""
    60→    record = make_adapter_record()
    61→    result = registry.store(record)
    62→    assert result is None
    63→
    64→
    65→# --- retrieve_by_id() ---
    66→
    67→
    68→def test_retrieve_by_id_returns_matching_record(registry, make_adapter_record) -> None:
    69→    """retrieve_by_id() returns the stored AdapterRecord."""
    70→    record = make_adapter_record(id="unique-adapter-xyz")
    71→    registry.store(record)
    72→    retrieved = registry.retrieve_by_id("unique-adapter-xyz")
    73→    assert retrieved.id == "unique-adapter-xyz"
    74→    assert retrieved.task_type == record.task_type
    75→
    76→
    77→def test_retrieve_by_id_raises_when_missing(registry) -> None:
    78→    """retrieve_by_id() raises AdapterNotFoundError for unknown ID."""
    79→    with pytest.raises(AdapterNotFoundError):
    80→        registry.retrieve_by_id("nonexistent-id")
    81→
    82→
    83→# --- query_by_task_type() ---
    84→
    85→
    86→def test_query_by_task_type_returns_matching_records(
    87→    registry, make_adapter_record
    88→) -> None:
    89→    """query_by_task_type() returns only records with the specified task_type."""
    90→    registry.store(make_adapter_record(id="a1", task_type="bug-fix"))
    91→    registry.store(make_adapter_record(id="a2", task_type="feature"))
    92→    registry.store(make_adapter_record(id="a3", task_type="bug-fix"))
    93→
    94→    results = registry.query_by_task_type("bug-fix")
    95→    assert len(results) == 2
    96→    assert all(r.task_type == "bug-fix" for r in results)
    97→
    98→
    99→def test_query_by_task_type_returns_empty_list_when_none(registry) -> None:
   100→    """query_by_task_type() returns empty list when no records match."""
   101→    results = registry.query_by_task_type("nonexistent-type")
   102→    assert results == []
   103→
   104→
   105→# --- list_all() ---
   106→
   107→
   108→def test_list_all_excludes_archived_records(registry, make_adapter_record) -> None:
   109→    """list_all() excludes records where is_archived=True."""
   110→    registry.store(make_adapter_record(id="active-1", is_archived=False))
   111→    registry.store(make_adapter_record(id="archived-1", is_archived=True))
   112→    registry.store(make_adapter_record(id="active-2", is_archived=False))
   113→
   114→    results = registry.list_all()
   115→    ids = [r.id for r in results]
   116→    assert "active-1" in ids
   117→    assert "active-2" in ids
   118→    assert "archived-1" not in ids
   119→
   120→
   121→def test_list_all_returns_only_non_archived(registry, make_adapter_record) -> None:
   122→    """list_all() returns list[AdapterRecord] with is_archived=False."""
   123→    registry.store(make_adapter_record(id="r1", is_archived=False))
   124→    registry.store(make_adapter_record(id="r2", is_archived=True))
   125→
   126→    results = registry.list_all()
   127→    assert len(results) == 1
   128→    assert results[0].id == "r1"
   129→
   130→
   131→def test_list_all_returns_empty_when_all_archived(
   132→    registry, make_adapter_record
   133→) -> None:
   134→    """list_all() returns empty list when all records are archived."""
   135→    registry.store(make_adapter_record(id="archived", is_archived=True))
   136→    results = registry.list_all()
   137→    assert results == []
   138→
   139→
   140→# --- WAL mode integration ---
   141→
   142→
   143→def test_wal_mode_enabled(tmp_path) -> None:
   144→    """AdapterRegistry enables WAL journal mode on file-based SQLite engine."""
   145→    from sqlalchemy import text
   146→
   147→    db_path = tmp_path / "test.db"
   148→    wal_engine = create_engine(f"sqlite:///{db_path}")
   149→    AdapterRegistry(engine=wal_engine)
   150→    with wal_engine.connect() as conn:
   151→        result = conn.execute(text("PRAGMA journal_mode")).scalar()
   152→    assert result == "wal"
   153→
   154→
   155→# --- Concurrent writes integration ---
   156→
   157→
   158→def test_concurrent_writes_no_deadlock(tmp_path, make_adapter_record) -> None:
   159→    """Five concurrent writes complete without deadlock or errors."""
   160→    import threading
   161→
   162→    db_path = tmp_path / "concurrent.db"
   163→    conc_engine = create_engine(f"sqlite:///{db_path}")
   164→    conc_registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=conc_engine)
   165→    errors: list[Exception] = []
   166→
   167→    def write(adapter_id: str) -> None:
   168→        try:
   169→            conc_registry.store(make_adapter_record(id=adapter_id))
   170→        except Exception as e:
   171→            errors.append(e)
   172→
   173→    threads = [threading.Thread(target=write, args=(f"t-{i}",)) for i in range(5)]
   174→    for t in threads:
   175→        t.start()
   176→    for t in threads:
   177→        t.join()
   178→
   179→    assert errors == [], f"Concurrent writes produced errors: {errors}"
   180→    assert len(conc_registry.list_all()) == 5
   181→
```

```
     1→"""Green-phase behavior tests for rune-agent node functions.
     2→
     3→Tests verify the actual behavior of all 4 node functions:
     4→generate_node, execute_node, reflect_node, save_trajectory_node.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from typing import Any
     8→from unittest.mock import AsyncMock, MagicMock, patch
     9→
    10→import pytest
    11→from inference import GenerationResult
    12→from rune_agent.nodes import (
    13→    execute_node,
    14→    generate_node,
    15→    reflect_node,
    16→    save_trajectory_node,
    17→)
    18→
    19→
    20→def _make_generation_result(text: str) -> GenerationResult:
    21→    """Helper to build a GenerationResult for mocking."""
    22→    return GenerationResult(
    23→        text=text,
    24→        model="test-model",
    25→        adapter_id=None,
    26→        token_count=10,
    27→        finish_reason="stop",
    28→    )
    29→
    30→
    31→def _mock_provider(text: str) -> MagicMock:
    32→    """Return a mock InferenceProvider whose generate() returns the given text."""
    33→    provider = MagicMock()
    34→    provider.generate = AsyncMock(return_value=_make_generation_result(text))
    35→    return provider
    36→
    37→
    38→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    39→# generate_node tests
    40→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    41→
    42→
    43→async def test_generate_node_first_attempt() -> None:
    44→    """generate_node first attempt: extracted code returned from python block."""
    45→    code_in_block = "def fib(n):\n    return n"
    46→    llm_response = f"```python\n{code_in_block}\n```"
    47→    provider = _mock_provider(llm_response)
    48→
    49→    state: dict[str, Any] = {
    50→        "task_description": "Write fibonacci",
    51→        "task_type": "function",
    52→        "test_suite": "assert fib(1) == 1",
    53→        "adapter_ids": [],
    54→        "attempt_count": 0,
    55→        "generated_code": "",
    56→        "stdout": "",
    57→        "stderr": "",
    58→        "exit_code": 0,
    59→    }
    60→
    61→    with patch("rune_agent.nodes.get_provider", return_value=provider):
    62→        result = await generate_node(state)
    63→
    64→    assert result["generated_code"] == code_in_block
    65→    provider.generate.assert_called_once()
    66→
    67→
    68→async def test_generate_node_retry_prompt_includes_errors() -> None:
    69→    """generate_node retry: prompt includes 'previous attempt' context."""
    70→    provider = _mock_provider("```python\nprint('fixed')\n```")
    71→
    72→    state: dict[str, Any] = {
    73→        "task_description": "Write fibonacci",
    74→        "task_type": "function",
    75→        "test_suite": "assert fib(5) == 5",
    76→        "adapter_ids": [],
    77→        "attempt_count": 1,
    78→        "generated_code": "def fib(n): return n",
    79→        "stdout": "",
    80→        "stderr": "AssertionError",
    81→        "exit_code": 1,
    82→    }
    83→
    84→    with patch("rune_agent.nodes.get_provider", return_value=provider):
    85→        await generate_node(state)
    86→
    87→    call_args = provider.generate.call_args
    88→    prompt_used = call_args.kwargs.get("prompt") or call_args.args[0]
    89→    assert "previous attempt" in prompt_used.lower()
    90→
    91→
    92→async def test_generate_node_no_code_block_fallback() -> None:
    93→    """generate_node: full response used when no python block present."""
    94→    raw_text = "def fib(n):\n    return n"
    95→    provider = _mock_provider(raw_text)
    96→
    97→    state: dict[str, Any] = {
    98→        "task_description": "Write fibonacci",
    99→        "task_type": "function",
   100→        "test_suite": "assert fib(1) == 1",
   101→        "adapter_ids": [],
   102→        "attempt_count": 0,
   103→        "generated_code": "",
   104→        "stdout": "",
   105→        "stderr": "",
   106→        "exit_code": 0,
   107→    }
   108→
   109→    with patch("rune_agent.nodes.get_provider", return_value=provider):
   110→        result = await generate_node(state)
   111→
   112→    assert result["generated_code"] == raw_text.strip()
   113→
   114→
   115→async def test_generate_node_with_adapter() -> None:
   116→    """generate_node: adapter_ids[0] passed as adapter_id when non-empty."""
   117→    provider = _mock_provider("```python\npass\n```")
   118→
   119→    state: dict[str, Any] = {
   120→        "task_description": "Write fibonacci",
   121→        "task_type": "function",
   122→        "test_suite": "assert True",
   123→        "adapter_ids": ["my-adapter"],
   124→        "attempt_count": 0,
   125→        "generated_code": "",
   126→        "stdout": "",
   127→        "stderr": "",
   128→        "exit_code": 0,
   129→    }
   130→
   131→    with patch("rune_agent.nodes.get_provider", return_value=provider):
   132→        await generate_node(state)
   133→
   134→    call_kwargs = provider.generate.call_args.kwargs
   135→    assert call_kwargs.get("adapter_id") == "my-adapter"
   136→
   137→
   138→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   139→# execute_node tests
   140→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   141→
   142→
   143→async def test_execute_node_passing_script() -> None:
   144→    """execute_node: simple passing script returns tests_passed=True, exit_code=0."""
   145→    state: dict[str, Any] = {
   146→        "generated_code": "x = 1",
   147→        "test_suite": "assert x == 1",
   148→    }
   149→    result = await execute_node(state)
   150→
   151→    assert result["tests_passed"] is True
   152→    assert result["exit_code"] == 0
   153→    assert isinstance(result["stdout"], str)
   154→    assert isinstance(result["stderr"], str)
   155→
   156→
   157→async def test_execute_node_failing_script() -> None:
   158→    """execute_node: failing assertion returns tests_passed=False, exit_code!=0."""
   159→    state: dict[str, Any] = {
   160→        "generated_code": "# intentionally empty",
   161→        "test_suite": "assert False, 'expected failure'",
   162→    }
   163→    result = await execute_node(state)
   164→
   165→    assert result["tests_passed"] is False
   166→    assert result["exit_code"] != 0
   167→    assert len(result["stderr"]) > 0
   168→
   169→
   170→async def test_execute_node_timeout(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
   171→    """execute_node: timeout returns error message in stderr, tests_passed=False."""
   172→    monkeypatch.setenv("RUNE_EXEC_TIMEOUT", "1")
   173→
   174→    state: dict[str, Any] = {
   175→        "generated_code": "import time; time.sleep(60)",
   176→        "test_suite": "",
   177→    }
   178→    result = await execute_node(state)
   179→
   180→    assert result["tests_passed"] is False
   181→    assert result["exit_code"] == 1
   182→    assert "timed out" in result["stderr"].lower()
   183→
   184→
   185→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   186→# reflect_node tests
   187→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   188→
   189→
   190→async def test_reflect_node_increments_and_appends() -> None:
   191→    """reflect_node: increments attempt_count and appends step to trajectory."""
   192→    state: dict[str, Any] = {
   193→        "attempt_count": 0,
   194→        "generated_code": "def fib(n): pass",
   195→        "stdout": "",
   196→        "stderr": "error",
   197→        "exit_code": 1,
   198→        "tests_passed": False,
   199→        "trajectory": [],
   200→    }
   201→    result = await reflect_node(state)
   202→
   203→    assert result["attempt_count"] == 1
   204→    assert len(result["trajectory"]) == 1
   205→    step = result["trajectory"][0]
   206→    assert step["generated_code"] == state["generated_code"]
   207→    assert step["stdout"] == state["stdout"]
   208→    assert step["stderr"] == state["stderr"]
   209→    assert step["exit_code"] == state["exit_code"]
   210→    assert step["tests_passed"] == state["tests_passed"]
   211→
   212→
   213→async def test_reflect_node_immutable_trajectory() -> None:
   214→    """reflect_node: original trajectory list is not mutated."""
   215→    original_trajectory: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
   216→    state: dict[str, Any] = {
   217→        "attempt_count": 2,
   218→        "generated_code": "x = 1",
   219→        "stdout": "ok",
   220→        "stderr": "",
   221→        "exit_code": 0,
   222→        "tests_passed": True,
   223→        "trajectory": original_trajectory,
   224→    }
   225→    result = await reflect_node(state)
   226→
   227→    # Original list must remain empty -- no in-place mutation
   228→    assert original_trajectory == []
   229→    assert len(result["trajectory"]) == 1
   230→
```

```
     1→"""Tests for evaluation.metrics module.

     3→Green-phase tests for calculate_pass_at_k, run_kill_switch_gate, and
     4→run_humaneval_subset. Wireframe NotImplementedError tests retained for

    24→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    25→# calculate_pass_at_k — green-phase tests
    26→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    27→
    28→
    29→def test_pass_at_k_perfect_score() -> None:
    30→    """calculate_pass_at_k(1, 1, 1) returns 1.0."""
    31→    assert calculate_pass_at_k(n_samples=1, n_correct=1, k=1) == 1.0
    32→
    33→
    34→def test_pass_at_k_zero_score() -> None:
    35→    """calculate_pass_at_k(1, 0, 1) returns 0.0."""
    36→    assert calculate_pass_at_k(n_samples=1, n_correct=0, k=1) == 0.0
    37→
    38→
    39→def test_pass_at_k_direct_rate() -> None:
    40→    """calculate_pass_at_k(100, 85, 1) returns 0.85 (n_samples>>k, pass@1 = c/n)."""
    41→    result = calculate_pass_at_k(n_samples=100, n_correct=85, k=1)
    42→    assert abs(result - 0.85) < 1e-9
    43→
    44→
    45→def test_pass_at_k_unbiased_estimator() -> None:
    46→    """calculate_pass_at_k(10, 5, 3) returns correct unbiased estimator value.
    47→
    48→    Formula: 1 - prod((n-c-i)/(n-i) for i in range(k))
    49→    n=10, c=5, k=3
    50→    term i=0: (10-5-0)/(10-0) = 5/10
    51→    term i=1: (10-5-1)/(10-1) = 4/9
    52→    term i=2: (10-5-2)/(10-2) = 3/8
    53→    prod = (5/10)*(4/9)*(3/8) = 60/720 = 1/12
    54→    pass@3 = 1 - 1/12 = 11/12
    55→    """
    56→    expected = 1 - (5 / 10) * (4 / 9) * (3 / 8)
    57→    result = calculate_pass_at_k(n_samples=10, n_correct=5, k=3)
    58→    assert abs(result - expected) < 1e-9
    59→
    60→
    61→def test_pass_at_k_raises_on_n_correct_gt_n_samples() -> None:
    62→    """calculate_pass_at_k raises ValueError when n_correct > n_samples."""
    63→    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
    64→        calculate_pass_at_k(n_samples=5, n_correct=10, k=1)
    65→

   132→HUMANEVAL_JSON = (

   141→def test_humaneval_json_exists() -> None:
   142→    """humaneval_subset.json file exists in the data directory."""
   143→    assert HUMANEVAL_JSON.exists(), f"Expected JSON at {HUMANEVAL_JSON}"

   145→
   146→def test_humaneval_json_has_20_tasks() -> None:
   147→    """humaneval_subset.json contains exactly 20 tasks."""
   148→    with HUMANEVAL_JSON.open() as f:
   149→        tasks = json.load(f)
   150→    assert len(tasks) == 20, f"Expected 20 tasks, got {len(tasks)}"
   151→
   152→
   153→def test_humaneval_json_task_fields() -> None:
   154→    """Each task has required fields: task_id, prompt, etc."""
   155→    with HUMANEVAL_JSON.open() as f:
   156→        tasks = json.load(f)
   157→    required = {"task_id", "prompt", "canonical_solution", "test", "entry_point"}
   158→    for task in tasks:
   159→        missing = required - task.keys()
   160→        assert not missing, f"Task {task.get('task_id', '?')} missing fields: {missing}"
   161→
   162→
   163→def test_humaneval_json_contains_task_0() -> None:
   164→    """humaneval_subset.json contains HumanEval/0."""
   165→    with HUMANEVAL_JSON.open() as f:
   166→        tasks = json.load(f)
   167→    task_ids = {t["task_id"] for t in tasks}
   168→    assert "HumanEval/0" in task_ids

   171→def test_run_humaneval_subset_baseline_returns_expected_keys() -> None:
   172→    """run_humaneval_subset returns dict with expected keys."""
   173→    # Use canonical solutions as completions for a subset
   174→    with HUMANEVAL_JSON.open() as f:
   175→        tasks = json.load(f)
   176→    # Just pass one task via completions
   177→    first_task = tasks[0]
   178→    completions = {first_task["task_id"]: first_task["canonical_solution"]}
   179→    result = run_humaneval_subset(adapter_id=None, completions=completions)
   180→    assert "pass_count" in result
   181→    assert "fail_count" in result
   182→    assert "pass_rate" in result
   183→    assert "task_results" in result
   184→
   185→
   186→def test_run_humaneval_subset_task_results_structure() -> None:
   187→    """task_results has one entry per task with task_id and passed boolean."""
   188→    with HUMANEVAL_JSON.open() as f:

   191→    completions = {first_task["task_id"]: first_task["canonical_solution"]}

   193→    assert len(result["task_results"]) == 1

   195→    assert "task_id" in tr
   196→    assert "passed" in tr
   197→    assert isinstance(tr["passed"], bool)
   198→
   199→
   200→def test_run_humaneval_subset_canonical_solution_passes() -> None:
   201→    """A task with correct canonical_solution passes the execution pipeline."""
   202→    with HUMANEVAL_JSON.open() as f:
   203→        tasks = json.load(f)
   204→    # HumanEval/0: has_close_elements — canonical solution should pass
   205→    task = next(t for t in tasks if t["task_id"] == "HumanEval/0")
   206→    completions = {task["task_id"]: task["canonical_solution"]}
   207→    result = run_humaneval_subset(adapter_id=None, completions=completions)
   208→    assert result["pass_count"] >= 1
   209→    passed_task = next(
   210→        t for t in result["task_results"] if t["task_id"] == "HumanEval/0"
   211→    )
   212→    assert passed_task["passed"] is True
   213→
   214→
   215→def test_run_humaneval_subset_wrong_completion_fails() -> None:
   216→    """An intentionally wrong completion results in a failed task."""
   217→    with HUMANEVAL_JSON.open() as f:

   224→        t for t in result["task_results"] if t["task_id"] == task["task_id"]
   225→    )
   226→    assert failed_task["passed"] is False
```

## Commit 25-02: Implement HardwareProbe + HardwareBudget

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_01EzqkLF68EK8FFET3x61iYq
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/shared/src/shared/hardware.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Hardware detection and resource budgeting for swarm execution.\n\nProvides HardwareProbe for detecting CPU, RAM, and GPU resources, and\nHardwareBudget for computing concurrency limits based on available hardware.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\n\nimport psutil\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass GPUInfo:\n    \"\"\"Information about a single GPU device.\n\n    Attributes:\n        name: Human-readable GPU name (e.g. 'NVIDIA RTX 4090').\n        vram_mb: Total VRAM in megabytes.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    vram_mb: int\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass HardwareBudget:\n    \"\"\"Concurrency limits derived from detected hardware.\n\n    Attributes:\n        max_agents: Maximum concurrent swarm agents.\n        max_concurrent_loras: Maximum LoRA adapters loaded at once.\n        training_slots: Number of concurrent training jobs (0 if no GPU).\n        vram_per_gpu_mb: VRAM available per GPU (0 if no GPU).\n        single_gpu_mode: True when only 0 or 1 GPUs are available.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    max_agents: int\n    max_concurrent_loras: int\n    training_slots: int\n    vram_per_gpu_mb: int\n    single_gpu_mode: bool\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass HardwareProbe:\n    \"\"\"Detected hardware resources on the current machine.\n\n    Attributes:\n        cpu_count: Number of logical CPU cores.\n        ram_total_mb: Total system RAM in megabytes.\n        gpus: List of detected GPU devices.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    cpu_count: int\n    ram_total_mb: int\n    gpus: list[GPUInfo] = field(default_factory=list)\n\n    @classmethod\n    def detect(cls) -> HardwareProbe:\n        \"\"\"Detect hardware resources on the current machine.\n\n        Uses psutil for CPU/RAM detection (always available). Attempts to\n        import pynvml for GPU detection; falls back to empty GPU list if\n        pynvml is not installed or NVML initialization fails.\n\n        Returns:\n            A HardwareProbe with detected resource information.\n        \"\"\"\n        cpu_count = psutil.cpu_count(logical=True) or 1\n        ram_total_mb = psutil.virtual_memory().total // (1024 * 1024)\n\n        gpus: list[GPUInfo] = []\n        try:\n            import pynvml\n\n            pynvml.nvmlInit()\n            device_count = pynvml.nvmlDeviceGetCount()\n            for i in range(device_count):\n                handle = pynvml.nvmlDeviceGetHandleByIndex(i)\n                name = pynvml.nvmlDeviceGetName(handle)\n                if isinstance(name, bytes):\n                    name = name.decode(\"utf-8\")\n                mem_info = pynvml.nvmlDeviceGetMemoryInfo(handle)\n                vram_mb = mem_info.total // (1024 * 1024)\n                gpus.append(GPUInfo(name=name, vram_mb=vram_mb))\n            pynvml.nvmlShutdown()\n        except (ImportError, Exception):\n            pass\n\n        return cls(cpu_count=cpu_count, ram_total_mb=ram_total_mb, gpus=gpus)\n\n    def compute_budget(self, base_model_vram_mb: int = 0) -> HardwareBudget:\n        \"\"\"Compute concurrency limits based on detected hardware.\n\n        Args:\n            base_model_vram_mb: VRAM consumed by the base inference model.\n                Used to calculate how much VRAM is left for LoRA adapters\n                and training.\n\n        Returns:\n            A HardwareBudget with computed concurrency limits.\n        \"\"\"\n        num_gpus = len(self.gpus)\n        single_gpu_mode = num_gpus <= 1\n\n        if num_gpus == 0:\n            return HardwareBudget(\n                max_agents=max(1, self.cpu_count // 2),\n                max_concurrent_loras=0,\n                training_slots=0,\n                vram_per_gpu_mb=0,\n                single_gpu_mode=True,\n            )\n\n        vram_per_gpu = self.gpus[0].vram_mb\n        free_vram = max(0, vram_per_gpu - base_model_vram_mb)\n\n        # Each LoRA adapter uses ~100MB VRAM\n        max_loras = max(1, free_vram // 100)\n\n        # Training needs ~4GB VRAM minimum\n        training_slots = 1 if free_vram >= 4096 else 0\n\n        # Agents limited by CPU cores and available VRAM\n        max_agents = max(1, min(self.cpu_count // 2, num_gpus * 4))\n\n        return HardwareBudget(\n            max_agents=max_agents,\n            max_concurrent_loras=max_loras,\n            training_slots=training_slots,\n            vram_per_gpu_mb=vram_per_gpu,\n            single_gpu_mode=single_gpu_mode,\n        )\n"
}
```
